B) Import and deploy the site
In Vercel: Add New… → Project → Import from GitHub → pick your repo.

Framework is auto-detected (Next.js). Keep defaults:

Install Command: npm ci (or npm install)

Build Command: npm run build

Output: .next

Click Deploy. You’ll get a preview URL like clipollo.vercel.app.

Note: our Rust daemon is local-only right now. The deployed site can just show a landing page or gracefully hide engine-only features until we host the engine later.

C) Add your domain (free HTTPS)
You can either keep DNS at Name.com or let Vercel host DNS. Both give you free SSL automatically.

Option 1 — Keep DNS at Name.com (simple, no move):

In Vercel: Settings → Domains → Add clipollo.com.

Choose “I’ll configure my own DNS”.

At Name.com DNS add:

A record: @ → 76.76.21.21

CNAME: www → cname.vercel-dns.com

Back on Vercel Domains page, wait for the green check. HTTPS cert is issued automatically.

In Vercel domain settings, set Redirect www → clipollo.com.

Option 2 — Move DNS to Vercel (they manage records):

In Vercel: add clipollo.com → choose Use Vercel DNS.

Vercel shows nameservers. Go to Name.com → change nameservers to those.

Vercel will handle A/CNAME and issue HTTPS automatically.

D) (Optional) Nice touches right away
Add app.clipollo.com later for the app, api.clipollo.com when the backend is online.

Short version: Yes—Vercel’s free Hobby plan is for non-commercial use. If you’re launching a real business (taking money, running a brand, ads, etc.), you should be on Pro (or pick another host whose free tier allows commercial use).

Your options
1) Stay on Vercel → use Pro
Easiest path, great DX for Next.js, automatic HTTPS.

Historically ~$20–$24 per seat/month (check current pricing before you click).

Keep in mind: our Rust daemon can’t run on Vercel. You’ll still host that elsewhere (Render/Fly.io/Hetzner/your VPS) and call it from the Next.js app.

2) Use a free host that allows commercial on the free tier
Cloudflare Pages (free) + Cloudflare DNS/SSL: commercial use is fine on the free plan; generous limits; easy custom domains/HTTPS.

Netlify Starter often works for small commercial sites, but double-check their latest plan terms for your usage.

You can always start on Cloudflare Pages for $0, then move to Vercel Pro later if you prefer their workflows.

Suggested rollout (balanced cost + speed)
Marketing site / web UI:

If you want $0 now → Cloudflare Pages (auto HTTPS, custom domain, commercial ok).

If you want Vercel features now → Vercel Pro.

Rust engine (daemon): host separately:

Render (cheap, simple), Fly.io, or a $5–$10/mo VPS (Hetzner/OVH/Linode).

Use Let’s Encrypt or Caddy for free HTTPS on the daemon, e.g. api.clipollo.com.

DNS: keep Name.com or move to Cloudflare. Either way, we’ll use free certificates (Let’s Encrypt/Cloudflare).

What I recommend for you right now
Since you’re cost-sensitive at the start:

Put the Next.js site on Cloudflare Pages (free, commercial-OK).

Stand up the Rust engine on Render/Fly/VPS behind api.clipollo.com.

When traffic/revenue warrant it, decide whether to move the app to Vercel Pro for DX/perf features.

If you’d rather just pay a little for simplicity, go straight to Vercel Pro and we’ll keep the daemon on Render/VPS.

Thought for 13s
Perfect—Cloudflare is a great $0 start that still gives you fast global CDN + free HTTPS. Here’s the exact path to get your Next.js app live on Cloudflare Pages and your clipollo.com pointing to it.

0) What we’re doing
Host the web app on Cloudflare Pages (free, commercial OK).

Point clipollo.com (and www.clipollo.com) to that Pages site.

Get free HTTPS automatically.

(Later) host the Rust engine separately at api.clipollo.com.

1) Create Cloudflare account + add your domain
Go to cloudflare.com → Sign Up (free).

Add a site → type clipollo.com → choose the Free plan.

Cloudflare shows you two nameservers (e.g., alice.ns.cloudflare.com, bob.ns.cloudflare.com).

At your registrar (Name.com), change the domain’s nameservers to those two Cloudflare values.

After you save, Cloudflare will say “Pending Nameserver Update.” It usually goes live within 5–30 minutes.

Once Cloudflare manages DNS, you get Universal SSL for free. No need to buy anything.

2) Connect GitHub and deploy Next.js to Pages
You’ll deploy the existing web/ project using Next on Pages (Cloudflare’s adapter for Next.js).

A) Update your repo (one-time)
From your repo root (video-platform), run:

cd web
npm i -D @cloudflare/next-on-pages wrangler @cloudflare/workers-types
Add these scripts to web/package.json:

{
  "scripts": {
    "build:cf": "npx @cloudflare/next-on-pages build",
    "preview:cf": "npx wrangler pages dev .vercel/output/static --compatibility-date=2024-09-01"
  }
}
(No other code changes needed right now. We’ll keep the site simple until the engine is hosted.)

Commit and push:

git add web/package.json
git commit -m "chore(web): add Cloudflare Pages build scripts"
git push
B) Create a Pages project
Cloudflare dashboard → Pages → Create a project.

Connect to Git → choose your GitHub repo → pick the web/ folder as project root (Cloudflare detects Next.js automatically, but we’ll set custom commands).

Build settings:

Build command: npx @cloudflare/next-on-pages build

Build output directory: .vercel/output/static

Functions directory: .vercel/output/functions

(Environment): set NODE_VERSION to 22 if there’s a field (optional; CF usually picks a compatible runtime)

Click Save and Deploy.
After the first build, you’ll get a URL like https://<project>.pages.dev/.

If it complains about not finding .vercel/output, double-check the build command and directory paths above.

3) Attach clipollo.com to your Pages project
Pages project → Custom domains → Set up a custom domain.

Add:

clipollo.com (apex)

www.clipollo.com

Cloudflare will auto-create the right DNS records (CNAME flattening for the apex, CNAME for www).

Wait for both to show Active with a green lock (HTTPS). This can be instant or a few minutes.

Want www → root redirect? In Rules → Redirect Rules, add:

If: Hostname equals www.clipollo.com

Then: Static redirect to https://clipollo.com (301)

4) Recommended free security toggles
Cloudflare dashboard → domain clipollo.com:

SSL/TLS → Overview: set to Full (or Flexible if you only use Pages for now; Full is fine).

SSL/TLS → Edge Certificates:

Always Use HTTPS: ON

Automatic HTTPS Rewrites: ON

(Later) HSTS: ON after you confirm everything is stable.

DNS → DNSSEC: enable DNSSEC (one click) once your nameservers are live.

5) About the engine (api.clipollo.com)
Cloudflare Pages is static/edge functions—not for running your Rust daemon. When you’re ready:

Host the Rust service on Render, Fly.io, or a small VPS (Hetzner/Linode).

Give it HTTPS using Caddy (auto TLS) or Let’s Encrypt (Certbot).

In Cloudflare DNS, create api → type A (VPS IP) or CNAME (host-provided endpoint).

We’ll update the Next.js app to call https://api.clipollo.com.

6) Quick smoke test
Open https://clipollo.com and https://www.clipollo.com

Confirm the padlock (HTTPS).

If you want a simple landing page, say the word—I can drop a clean hero page into web/ with your Clipollo logo and colors so your first deploy looks polished.

If you hit any step that looks different in your dashboard, tell me exactly what you see and I’ll adjust the instructions.

Do this now (5–10 minutes)

Choose Use custom nameservers and paste the two from Cloudflare.

Back in Cloudflare, click “Done, check nameservers.”

Status will be Pending for a bit (5–30 min, sometimes up to a few hours).

You’ll get an email when the domain is “Active”.

Once it flips to Active, Cloudflare’s Universal SSL is issued automatically for the domain.

After activation: hook up the website

clipollo.com

www.clipollo.com

Cloudflare will create the DNS records for you (don’t add them manually).

C) Clean up old records

You said:
the name.com have 4 nameservers, cloudflare has 2?
ChatGPT said:
Totally normal. Registrars (like Name.com) often show 4 nameserver slots, while Cloudflare assigns you exactly 2. You should use only the two Cloudflare nameservers they gave you.

What to do at Name.com
Go to clipollo.com → Manage Nameservers.

Choose Use custom nameservers.

Enter only the two Cloudflare NS from your Cloudflare Overview → “Assigned nameservers” (e.g., mila.ns.cloudflare.com, raj.ns.cloudflare.com).

Delete any other nameservers in the extra slots (leave them blank).

If the UI insists on four, just put the same two in the first two slots and leave the other slots empty if allowed. Do not mix in non-Cloudflare NS.

Important notes
Don’t mix Cloudflare NS with old ones—authority becomes inconsistent.

If DNSSEC is enabled at Name.com, disable it before switching. After Cloudflare is active, enable DNSSEC in Cloudflare (free).

Propagation usually takes 5–30 minutes (occasionally a few hours). Cloudflare will show Active once it sees the change and then issue your free SSL.

Once you’ve switched them, tell me when Cloudflare shows Active and we’ll finish hooking up Pages + the custom domain.

Thought for 34s
Great news on Cloudflare being active! 👏

1) WHOIS privacy
Cloudflare does not provide WHOIS privacy for domains registered at other registrars.

If you transfer the domain to Cloudflare Registrar, they include free WHOIS redaction for supported TLDs.

Since you’re on Name.com, WHOIS privacy is handled (and sold) by Name.com. It’s optional—GDPR already redacts a lot for most lookups.

2) Are we using React?
Yes. Next.js is React (Next is the framework; React is the UI library).
So a “drop-in visual editor for React” will work in a Next.js app.

3) Install the UI Builder (works with Next.js + Tailwind + shadcn/ui)
UI Builder is here: uibuilder.app (open-source by olliethedev). It expects Tailwind + shadcn/ui and runs on the client (not Server Components). We’ll add it to a client route like /studio.

A) From your web/ folder, add shadcn + the UI Builder block registry
They recommend a specific CLI version while Tailwind v4/React 19 is still settling.

cd I:\Documents\Projects\MyVideoPlatformProject\video-platform\web

:: If you haven’t initialized shadcn/ui in this project yet:
npx shadcn@latest init

:: Add the UI Builder components via its registry
npx shadcn@2.1.8 add https://raw.githubusercontent.com/olliethedev/ui-builder/main/registry/block-registry.json
B) Fix dev dependencies (per their README)
npm install -D @types/lodash.template @tailwindcss/typography @types/react-syntax-highlighter tailwindcss-animate @types/object-hash zod
C) Tailwind config (if not already)
Open tailwind.config.js and ensure the plugin is added:

// tailwind.config.js
module.exports = {
  content: ["./app/**/*.{ts,tsx}", "./components/**/*.{ts,tsx}"],
  theme: { extend: {} },
  plugins: [require("tailwindcss-animate"), require("@tailwindcss/typography")],
};
D) Create a client route for the editor
Create app/studio/page.tsx with this full paste-in:

// app/studio/page.tsx
"use client";

import React from "react";

// UI Builder core (installed by the shadcn registry add)
import UIBuilder from "@/components/ui/ui-builder";

// Prebuilt component definitions so you don’t have to hand-register everything
import { primitiveComponentDefinitions } from "@/lib/ui-builder/registry/primitive-component-definitions";
import { complexComponentDefinitions } from "@/lib/ui-builder/registry/complex-component-definitions";

import type { ComponentRegistry } from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/types";

const componentRegistry: ComponentRegistry = {
  ...primitiveComponentDefinitions,   // div, span, img, etc.
  ...complexComponentDefinitions,     // Button, Card, Badge, etc. (shadcn/ui)
  // You can add your own components later here
};

export default function StudioPage() {
  return (
    <main className="min-h-screen p-6">
      <h1 className="text-2xl font-semibold mb-4">Clipollo Studio</h1>
      <UIBuilder componentRegistry={componentRegistry} />
    </main>
  );
}
Notes:

UI Builder’s live canvas must run client-side, so keep "use client" at the top.

Server Components (RSC) aren’t supported for the live editor preview (final render of saved JSON can be done server-side later if you want).

E) Run locally
npm run dev
Open http://localhost:3000/studio — you should see the editor.

F) Deploy on Cloudflare Pages later
Your existing Pages setup will build it with:

Build command: npx @cloudflare/next-on-pages build

Output dir: .vercel/output/static

Functions dir: .vercel/output/functions

Add the custom domains in Pages (clipollo.com, www.clipollo.com) and you’ll get free HTTPS.

If you hit an error anywhere (especially around Tailwind/shadcn versions), paste the console output and I’ll tweak the steps for your exact setup.

    "baseColor": "stone"

  "aliases": {
    "components": "@/components",

Good catch — this error is because our project is on React 19 (Next.js 15), while one of the UI Builder deps (he-tree-react@1.2.0) declares a peer dependency on React 18. NPM stops when peers don’t match.

You’ve got two workable paths:

Option A (fast, good for prototyping): ignore the peer clash
Re-run the add command but skip auto-install so it scaffolds files without trying to install packages:

Now install everything with NPM’s legacy peer mode (it will allow React 19 with those React-18 peers):

npm install --legacy-peer-deps
If you still see peer warnings, it’s fine for dev. Start the app:

This is the quickest way to try the editor. If later a lib actually breaks under React 19, we can swap/patch that one package. For now it usually works.

Which should you pick?
Pick Option A right now. It’s fastest and usually fine while we prototype the editor.

replace it (UI Builder uses it mainly for a tree/outline; there are React-19 friendly alternatives), or

temporarily pin the app to React 18 (Option B) until UI Builder’s deps update.

ChatGPT said:

Thought for 54s
You’re super close—the error is just a path change inside react-syntax-highlighter.

Why it’s failing
After the npm audit fix, your project pulled a newer react-syntax-highlighter that ships styles under dist/esm/styles/prism (ESM).
The UI-Builder file still imports the old CJS path dist/cjs/styles/prism, so Next can’t resolve it.

Fix (2 quick steps)
Ensure the package is present at a recent version:

cd /d "I:\Documents\Projects\MyVideoPlatformProject\video-platform\web"
npm i react-syntax-highlighter@15 --legacy-peer-deps
Replace the UI-Builder’s codeblock.tsx with this version (uses the ESM styles path):

components/ui/ui-builder/components/codeblock.tsx

"use client";

import React, { memo, useCallback, useMemo, useState } from "react";
import { Prism as SyntaxHighlighter } from "react-syntax-highlighter";
// NOTE: ESM path (newer versions). The old CJS path was: dist/cjs/styles/prism
import { coldarkDark } from "react-syntax-highlighter/dist/esm/styles/prism";

import { CopyIcon, CheckIcon } from "lucide-react";
import { useCopyToClipboard } from "@/hooks/use-copy-to-clipboard";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";

type CodeBlockProps = {
  code: string;
  language?: string;
  showCopy?: boolean;
  className?: string;
};

const CodeBlock = ({
  code,
  language = "tsx",
  showCopy = true,
  className = "",
}: CodeBlockProps) => {
  const normalized = useMemo(() => (code ?? "").trim(), [code]);
  const { copy } = useCopyToClipboard();
  const [copied, setCopied] = useState(false);

  const handleCopy = useCallback(async () => {
    await copy(normalized);
    setCopied(true);
    setTimeout(() => setCopied(false), 1600);
  }, [copy, normalized]);

  return (
    <div className={`relative rounded-md overflow-hidden ${className}`}>
      {showCopy && (
        <div className="absolute right-2 top-2 z-10">
          <Button size="sm" variant="secondary" onClick={handleCopy}>
            {copied ? (
              <>
                <CheckIcon className="h-4 w-4 mr-1" />
                Copied
              </>
            ) : (
              <>
                <CopyIcon className="h-4 w-4 mr-1" />
                Copy
              </>
            )}
          </Button>
        </div>
      )}

      <SyntaxHighlighter
        language={language}
        style={coldarkDark}
        customStyle={{
          margin: 0,
          padding: "16px",
          background: "transparent",
        }}
        codeTagProps={{ style: { fontSize: 13, lineHeight: 1.5 } }}
      >
        {normalized}
      </SyntaxHighlighter>
    </div>
  );
};

export default memo(CodeBlock);
Run again
npm run dev
Open http://localhost:3000/studio

About that “unknown utility class rounded” message
Tailwind v4 shows that when a CSS file (not JSX) uses Tailwind utilities via @apply without referencing Tailwind. If it pops up again, add this to the top of the offending .css file:

@reference "tailwindcss";
(You don’t need this in globals.css—only in CSS Modules or other imported CSS that use @apply.)

If anything else errors, paste the new message and I’ll patch it fast.

"use client";

import React, { memo, useCallback, useMemo, useState } from "react";
import { Prism as SyntaxHighlighter } from "react-syntax-highlighter";
// Use ESM styles path (newer versions). Old CJS path was: dist/cjs/styles/prism
import { coldarkDark } from "react-syntax-highlighter/dist/esm/styles/prism";

import { CopyIcon, CheckIcon } from "lucide-react";
import { useCopyToClipboard } from "@/hooks/use-copy-to-clipboard";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";

type CodeBlockProps = {
  code: string;
  language?: string;
  showCopy?: boolean;
  className?: string;
};

const CodeBlock = ({
  code,
  language = "tsx",
  showCopy = true,
  className = "",
}: CodeBlockProps) => {
  const normalized = useMemo(() => (code ?? "").trim(), [code]);
  const { copy } = useCopyToClipboard();
  const [copied, setCopied] = useState(false);

  const handleCopy = useCallback(async () => {
    await copy(normalized);
    setCopied(true);
    setTimeout(() => setCopied(false), 1600);
  }, [copy, normalized]);

  return (
    <div className={`relative rounded-md overflow-hidden ${className}`}>
      {showCopy && (
        <div className="absolute right-2 top-2 z-10">
          <Button size="sm" variant="secondary" onClick={handleCopy}>
            {copied ? (
              <>
                <CheckIcon className="h-4 w-4 mr-1" />
                Copied
              </>
            ) : (
              <>
                <CopyIcon className="h-4 w-4 mr-1" />
                Copy
              </>
            )}
          </Button>
        </div>
      )}

      <SyntaxHighlighter
        language={language}
        style={coldarkDark}
        customStyle={{ margin: 0, padding: "16px", background: "transparent" }}
        codeTagProps={{ style: { fontSize: 13, lineHeight: 1.5 } }}
      >
        {normalized}
      </SyntaxHighlighter>
    </div>
  );
};

export default memo(CodeBlock);

Thought for 16s
Nice progress — the editor route is up and we’ve cleared the syntax-highlighter issue. The new runtime error is:

cn is not a function (coming from components/ui/button.tsx)

Why it’s happening
UI-Builder’s registry imported cn from an internal helper path, but in our project the real cn helper lives in lib/utils.ts (created by shadcn init). So the import points to the wrong module and you get a non-function.

Fix (2–3 minutes)
Install helpers (just in case)

cd /d "I:\Documents\Projects\MyVideoPlatformProject\video-platform\web"
npm i clsx tailwind-merge --legacy-peer-deps
Point imports to our cn
Open this file:

web\components\ui\button.tsx
At the top you’ll see an import like:

import { cn } from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/internal/form-fields/classname-control/utils";
Replace it with:

import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";
Save.

Find any other wrong cn imports and fix them the same way
Run this to list files that reference the bad path:

cd /d "I:\Documents\Projects\MyVideoPlatformProject\video-platform\web"
findstr /s /m /c:"classname-control/utils" components\*.ts components\*.tsx components\**\*.ts components\**\*.tsx
Open each file it prints and change:

from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/internal/form-fields/classname-control/utils"
to:

from "@/lib/utils"
(From the stack you shared, likely candidates include:

components/ui/button.tsx

components/ui/ui-builder/internal/components/nav.tsx

components/ui/tooltip.tsx
…but the findstr command will tell us for sure.)

Restart dev server

npm run dev
Open http://localhost:3000/studio again.

If you still see a cn error
Double-check web\lib\utils.ts contains the standard shadcn helper:

import { type ClassValue, clsx } from "clsx"
import { twMerge } from "tailwind-merge"

export function cn(...inputs: ClassValue[]) {
  return twMerge(clsx(inputs))
}
If it’s missing, paste that in, save, and re-run.

Send me the next error (if any) and I’ll patch it fast.

1) “A tree hydrated but… didn’t match” (layout hydration)
Cause: the server renders <html> with className="dark" / style={{ colorScheme: 'dark' }} but the client theme may differ on first paint. Fix by letting the client control theme and telling React to ignore the first render mismatch.

Do this
Ensure next-themes is installed (it should be already, but run anyway):

cd /d "I:\Documents\Projects\MyVideoPlatformProject\video-platform\web"
npm i next-themes --legacy-peer-deps
Create a tiny wrapper: components/theme-provider.tsx

"use client";

import * as React from "react";
import { ThemeProvider as NextThemesProvider } from "next-themes";

export function ThemeProvider({
  children,
  ...props
}: React.ComponentProps<typeof NextThemesProvider>) {
  return <NextThemesProvider {...props}>{children}</NextThemesProvider>;
}
Update app/layout.tsx so the client owns theme classes and hydration is suppressed:

// app/layout.tsx
import "./globals.css";
import type { Metadata } from "next";
import { Geist, Geist_Mono } from "next/font/google";
import { ThemeProvider } from "@/components/theme-provider";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

const geistSans = Geist({ subsets: ["latin"], variable: "--font-geist-sans" });
const geistMono = Geist_Mono({ subsets: ["latin"], variable: "--font-geist-mono" });

export const metadata: Metadata = {
  title: "Clipollo",
  description: "Community-hosted video platform",
};

export default function RootLayout({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
  return (
    <html lang="en" suppressHydrationWarning>
      <body
        className={cn(geistSans.variable, geistMono.variable, "antialiased")}
      >
        <ThemeProvider attribute="class" defaultTheme="system" enableSystem>
          {children}
        </ThemeProvider>
      </body>
    </html>
  );
}
Notes:

We removed any static className="dark" or style={{colorScheme:'dark'}} on <html>.

suppressHydrationWarning prevents the one-time mismatch warning.

import { type ClassValue, clsx } from "clsx";
import { twMerge } from "tailwind-merge";

export function cn(...inputs: ClassValue[]) {
  return twMerge(clsx(inputs));

The two issues to fix tomorrow (quick notes so you’re unblocked fast)
1) frameRef warning on <iframe>
React DOM tags don’t accept a frameRef prop—use ref. If a component in the UI Builder expects a frameRef, adapt it:

Option A: use a tiny adapter

// components/IframeAdapter.tsx
import * as React from "react";

type IframeProps = React.ComponentPropsWithoutRef<"iframe">;

export function IframeAdapter({
  frameRef,
  ...props
}: IframeProps & { frameRef?: React.Ref<HTMLIFrameElement> }) {
  return <iframe ref={frameRef} {...props} />;
}
Usage:

const frameRef = React.useRef<HTMLIFrameElement>(null);
<IframeAdapter frameRef={frameRef} /* ...props */ />
Option B: standard forwardRef (preferred if you control the caller)

// components/CanvasFrame.tsx
import * as React from "react";

type IframeProps = React.ComponentPropsWithoutRef<"iframe">;

const CanvasFrame = React.forwardRef<HTMLIFrameElement, IframeProps>(
  (props, ref) => <iframe ref={ref} {...props} />
);
CanvasFrame.displayName = "CanvasFrame";

export default CanvasFrame;
Usage:

const frameRef = React.useRef<HTMLIFrameElement>(null);
<CanvasFrame ref={frameRef} /* ... */ />
Either way, remove frameRef from the DOM element itself.

2) Hydration mismatch on <body> (the Grammarly special)
Your screenshot shows attributes like data-gr-ext-installed and data-new-gr-c-s-check-loaded=*—those are injected by the Grammarly browser extension and frequently cause hydration warnings in Next.js dev.

Fastest fixes

Disable Grammarly on localhost:3000 (best dev experience), and reload.

Also add a couple of safe tweaks to suppress noise:

// app/layout.tsx
import "./globals.css";
import { ThemeProvider } from "next-themes";
import { GeistSans, GeistMono } from "geist/font/*"; // adjust to your import

export default function RootLayout({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
  return (
    <html lang="en" suppressHydrationWarning>
      <body
        suppressHydrationWarning
        data-gramm="false"           // tell Grammarly to leave it alone
        className={`${GeistSans.variable} ${GeistMono.variable} antialiased`}
      >
        <ThemeProvider
          attribute="class"
          defaultTheme="system"
          enableSystem
          disableTransitionOnChange
        >
          {children}
        </ThemeProvider>
      </body>
    </html>
  );
}
If you still see it with Grammarly off, ping me—we’ll check for any Date.now(), random IDs, or client-only code running during SSR.

// components/ui/ui-builder/components/iframe-adapter.tsx
import * as React from "react";

type IframeProps = React.ComponentPropsWithoutRef<"iframe">;

export function IframeAdapter({
  frameRef,
  ...props
}: IframeProps & { frameRef?: React.Ref<HTMLIFrameElement> }) {
  return <iframe ref={frameRef} {...props} />;
}

export default IframeAdapter;
“Usage” — where does that go?
We need to replace wherever the UI Builder renders <iframe frameRef={...} ... />.
In VS Code press Ctrl+Shift+F and search for:

frameRef=

or <iframe

You’ll likely find it under one of:

Then change that spot from:

to:

// AFTER
import IframeAdapter from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/components/iframe-adapter";

<IframeAdapter frameRef={someRef} /* other props */ />
If you don’t see frameRef anywhere and it’s actually the prop being passed down (not used directly), fix the component that receives frameRef and forwards it to <iframe>—replace its <iframe frameRef=...> with the adapter as above.

Fast fixes (do these in order)
Trust your workspace

(Copilot Chat won’t run in restricted mode.)

Update everything

Extensions: update GitHub Copilot and GitHub Copilot Chat.

Full sign-out / sign-in

Bottom-left Accounts icon → Sign out of GitHub (and any “Copilot” sessions).

Ctrl+Shift+P → GitHub: Sign out (and GitHub Copilot: Sign out if it’s there).

Ctrl+Shift+P → Developer: Reload Window.

Ctrl+Shift+P → GitHub: Sign in → your browser opens.
When the browser shows a prompt to “Open Visual Studio Code”, click Open (don’t ignore this; it’s what completes the login).

Back in VS Code, open the Copilot Chat view; it should show your GitHub account at the top.

Make sure the URL handler is working

If the browser opened but VS Code never popped back, the vscode:// handler may be blocked.

Try another browser (Chrome/Edge usually work best).

Or copy the long vscode://vscode.github-authentication/... link from the browser and paste it into Start → Run on Windows.

Worst case: reinstall VS Code (64-bit System Installer) to re-register the URL protocol.

Credential cache reset (only if it still loops)

vscode.github-authentication

github.com / login (GitHub tokens)

Restart VS Code and repeat step 3.

Network / proxy check

Allow these domains through firewall/VPN:

github.com, api.github.com, vscode-auth.github.com,

copilot-proxy.githubusercontent.com, chat.github.com

If you’re on Brave, temporarily disable aggressive blocking or use Edge/Chrome for the login handoff.

GitHub Authentication

GitHub Copilot

Absolutely—here’s a safe PowerShell script that will:

scan your web project for any DOM <iframe … frameRef=…> uses,

replace just those tags with your IframeAdapter,

fix the matching closing tags,

and auto-add the import
import IframeAdapter from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/components/iframe-adapter";
if it’s missing.

It makes a .bak backup of each changed file and prints a summary.

Script: fix-iframe-refs.ps1
Save this file inside your web folder (same level as app/ and components/), e.g.
I:\Documents\Projects\MyVideoPlatformProject\video-platform\web\fix-iframe-refs.ps1

# fix-iframe-refs.ps1
# Replaces <iframe ... frameRef=...> with <IframeAdapter ...> and fixes closing tags.
# Adds import IframeAdapter if missing. Creates .bak backups of modified files.

param(
  [switch]$DryRun
)

$root = Get-Location
Write-Host "[info] Scanning from $root" -ForegroundColor Cyan

# Helper: insert import after last import line block
function Add-Import-If-Missing {
  param(
    [string]$Content,
    [string]$ImportLine
  )

  if ($Content -match [regex]::Escape($ImportLine)) {
    return $Content
  }

  $lines = $Content -split "`r?`n"

  # find the last contiguous import line at top
  $lastImportIndex = -1
  for ($i = 0; $i -lt $lines.Count; $i++) {
    if ($lines[$i] -match '^\s*import\s.+;?\s*$') {
      $lastImportIndex = $i
      continue
    }
    # stop scanning once we hit the first non-import after seeing imports
    if ($lastImportIndex -ge 0 -and $lines[$i] -notmatch '^\s*import\s') {
      break
    }
  }

  if ($lastImportIndex -ge 0) {
    $before = $lines[0..$lastImportIndex] -join "`n"
    $after  = $lines[($lastImportIndex+1)..($lines.Count-1)] -join "`n"
    return ($before + "`n" + $ImportLine + "`n" + $after)
  } else {
    # no imports at top — prepend
    return ($ImportLine + "`n" + $Content)
  }
}

# Regex options
$singleline = [System.Text.RegularExpressions.RegexOptions]::Singleline
$multiline  = [System.Text.RegularExpressions.RegexOptions]::Multiline

# Only scan TSX files under app/ and components/ (skip node_modules/.next)
$files = Get-ChildItem -Recurse -File -Include *.tsx |
  Where-Object { $_.FullName -notmatch '\\node_modules\\' -and $_.FullName -notmatch '\\\.next\\' }

if ($files.Count -eq 0) {
  Write-Host "[warn] No .tsx files found. Are you in the web/ folder?" -ForegroundColor Yellow
  exit 0
}

$changed = @()
foreach ($f in $files) {
  $text = Get-Content $f.FullName -Raw

  # 1) Replace opening tags that have a frameRef prop:
  # Change ONLY <iframe ...> that contain frameRef= somewhere inside the same tag.
  $openPattern = '<iframe(?=[^>]*\bframeRef=)'
  $new = [regex]::Replace($text, $openPattern, '<IframeAdapter')

  $modified = $false
  if ($new -ne $text) {
    $modified = $true
    # 2) For any replaced opening tag, fix the matching closing tag(s).
    # Change </iframe> that closes those sections into </IframeAdapter>.
    # This broad replace is safe because only files that had an opening replacement get here.
    $new = [regex]::Replace($new, '</iframe\s*>', '</IframeAdapter>', $singleline)
    # 2b) Also handle the case of a non-self-closing opening pair in one pass:
    $new = [regex]::Replace($new, '<IframeAdapter\b([^>]*)>(.*?)</iframe\s*>', '<IframeAdapter$1>$2</IframeAdapter>', $singleline)

    # 3) Ensure the import exists
    $importLine = 'import IframeAdapter from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/components/iframe-adapter";'
    $new = Add-Import-If-Missing -Content $new -ImportLine $importLine
  }

  if ($modified) {
    if ($DryRun) {
      Write-Host "[dry-run] Would fix: $($f.FullName)" -ForegroundColor Yellow
      $changed += $f.FullName
    } else {
      Copy-Item $f.FullName ($f.FullName + ".bak") -Force
      Set-Content $f.FullName $new -Encoding UTF8
      Write-Host "[fixed] $($f.FullName)" -ForegroundColor Green
      $changed += $f.FullName
    }
  }
}

if ($changed.Count -eq 0) {
  Write-Host "[info] No files needed changes (no <iframe ... frameRef=...> found)." -ForegroundColor Cyan
} else {
  Write-Host "`n[summary] Updated $($changed.Count) file(s):" -ForegroundColor Cyan
  $changed | ForEach-Object { Write-Host " - $_" }
  Write-Host "`nBackups saved as *.bak next to each file." -ForegroundColor DarkGray
}

Write-Host "`nDone."
How to run it

# scan-frameref.ps1
param(
  [switch]$IncludeNodeModules
)

$here = Get-Location
Write-Host "[scan] Root: $here" -ForegroundColor Cyan

# Build include globs
$globs = @("*.ts","*.tsx","*.js","*.jsx")
$dirs  = @("app","components","lib","hooks","pages")

if ($IncludeNodeModules) {
  $dirs += "node_modules"
}

function Find-In {
  param([string]$pattern)
  $files = @()
  foreach ($d in $dirs) {
    if (Test-Path $d) {
      $files += Get-ChildItem -Recurse -File -Path $d -Include $globs -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
    }
  }
  if ($files.Count -eq 0) { return @() }
  try {
    $matches = $files | Select-String -Pattern $pattern -CaseSensitive -SimpleMatch
  } catch {
    $matches = @()
  }
  return $matches
}

# 1) Any text "frameRef"
$hits1 = Find-In "frameRef"

# 2) Any DOM iframes (<iframe)
#   Use regex so we don’t match <IframeAdapter>.
$hits2 = @()
foreach ($d in $dirs) {
  if (Test-Path $d) {
    $hits2 += Get-ChildItem -Recurse -File -Path $d -Include $globs `
      | Select-String -Pattern '<\s*iframe\b' -CaseSensitive
  }
}

Write-Host "`n=== frameRef matches ===" -ForegroundColor Yellow
if ($hits1.Count -eq 0) {
  Write-Host "(none)"
} else {
  $hits1 | Sort-Object Path, LineNumber `
    | ForEach-Object { "{0}:{1}: {2}" -f $_.Path, $_.LineNumber, $_.Line.Trim() } `
    | ForEach-Object { Write-Host $_ }
}

Write-Host "`n=== <iframe> occurrences ===" -ForegroundColor Yellow
if ($hits2.Count -eq 0) {
  Write-Host "(none)"
} else {
  $hits2 | Sort-Object Path, LineNumber `
    | ForEach-Object { "{0}:{1}: {2}" -f $_.Path, $_.LineNumber, $_.Line.Trim() } `
    | ForEach-Object { Write-Host $_ }
}

Write-Host "`nTip: To include node_modules, run: powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\scan-frameref.ps1 -IncludeNodeModules" -ForegroundColor DarkGray

2) Fix (getBaseType) is not a function crash

At line ~244 you call getBaseType(fieldDef as z.ZodAny) and crash because getBaseType isn’t a function. We’ll define a tiny local helper that unwraps Zod wrapper types and returns the base _def.typeName safely, then use it.

Do this at the top of props-panel.tsx (with other imports)
Add z import if it isn’t already present, and define the shim:

import { z } from "zod";

/** Safely unwrap Zod wrappers (effects/optional/default/etc.) to the underlying base type */
function getBaseType(schema: z.ZodAny): z.ZodFirstPartyTypeKind | undefined {
  let s: any = schema;
  // Unwrap common wrappers
  while (s?._def?.schema) {
    s = s._def.schema;
  }
  return s?._def?.typeName as z.ZodFirstPartyTypeKind | undefined;
}
Leave your existing usage unchanged:

const baseType = getBaseType(fieldDef as z.ZodAny);
This guarantees getBaseType exists and behaves as expected, eliminating the runtime error you’re hitting at props-panel.tsx:244.

✅ 1) auto-frame.tsx (full replacement)
You renamed the adapter to iframeadapter.tsx, so I kept that import. Your current file is also missing the final }); that closes the forwardRef callback—this version fixes that and keeps all of your logic intact.

Path: web/components/ui/ui-builder/internal/canvas/auto-frame.tsx

// web/components/ui/ui-builder/internal/canvas/auto-frame.tsx
import {
  createContext,
  ReactNode,
  useCallback,
  useContext,
  useEffect,
  useMemo,
  useState,
  forwardRef,
} from "react";
import hash from "object-hash";
import { createPortal } from "react-dom";
// NOTE: you said you renamed the adapter file to "iframeadapter.tsx"
import IframeAdapter from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/components/iframeadapter";

const styleSelector = 'style, link[rel="stylesheet"]';

const collectStyles = (doc: Document) => {
  const collected: HTMLElement[] = [];
  doc.querySelectorAll(styleSelector).forEach((style) => {
    collected.push(style as HTMLElement);
  });
  return collected;
};

const getStyleSheet = (el: HTMLElement) => {
  return Array.from(document.styleSheets).find((ss) => {
    const ownerNode = ss.ownerNode as HTMLLinkElement;
    return ownerNode.href === (el as HTMLLinkElement).href;
  });
};

const getStyles = (styleSheet?: CSSStyleSheet) => {
  if (styleSheet) {
    try {
      return Array.from(styleSheet.cssRules)
        .map((rule) => rule.cssText)
        .join("");
    } catch (e: any) {
      console.warn(
        "Access to stylesheet %s is denied. Ignoring…",
        styleSheet.href,
        e.message
      );
    }
  }
  return "";
};

// Sync attributes from parent window to iFrame
const syncAttributes = (sourceElement: Element, targetElement: Element) => {
  const attributes = sourceElement.attributes;
  if (attributes?.length > 0) {
    Array.from(attributes).forEach((attribute: Attr) => {
      targetElement.setAttribute(attribute.name, attribute.value);
    });
  }
};

const defer = (fn: () => void) => setTimeout(fn, 0);

const CopyHostStyles = ({
  children,
  debug = false,
  onStylesLoaded = () => null,
}: {
  children: ReactNode;
  debug?: boolean;
  onStylesLoaded?: () => void;
}) => {
  const { document: doc, window: win } = useFrame();

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!win || !doc) {
      return () => {};
    }

    const elements: { original: HTMLElement; mirror: HTMLElement }[] = [];
    const hashes: Record<string, boolean> = {};

    const lookupEl = (el: HTMLElement) =>
      elements.findIndex((elementMap) => elementMap.original === el);

    const mirrorEl = async (el: HTMLElement, inlineStyles = false) => {
      let mirror: HTMLStyleElement;

      if (el.nodeName === "LINK" && inlineStyles) {
        mirror = document.createElement("style") as HTMLStyleElement;
        mirror.type = "text/css";

        let styleSheet = getStyleSheet(el);

        if (!styleSheet) {
          await new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
            const fn = () => {
              resolve();
              el.removeEventListener("load", fn);
            };
            el.addEventListener("load", fn);
          });
          styleSheet = getStyleSheet(el);
        }

        const styles = getStyles(styleSheet);

        if (!styles) {
          if (debug) {
            console.warn(
              `Tried to load styles for link element, but couldn't find them. Skipping...`
            );
          }
          return;
        }

        mirror.innerHTML = styles;
        mirror.setAttribute("data-href", el.getAttribute("href")!);
      } else {
        mirror = el.cloneNode(true) as HTMLStyleElement;
      }

      return mirror;
    };

    const addEl = async (el: HTMLElement) => {
      const index = lookupEl(el);
      if (index > -1) {
        if (debug)
          console.log(
            `Tried to add an element that was already mirrored. Updating instead...`
          );
        elements[index].mirror.innerText = el.innerText;
        return;
      }

      const mirror = await mirrorEl(el);
      if (!mirror) return;

      const elHash = hash(mirror.outerHTML);
      if (hashes[elHash]) {
        if (debug)
          console.log(
            `iframe already contains element that is being mirrored. Skipping...`
          );
        return;
      }

      hashes[elHash] = true;

      doc.head.append(mirror as HTMLElement);
      elements.push({ original: el, mirror: mirror });

      if (debug) console.log(`Added style node ${el.outerHTML}`);
    };

    const removeEl = (el: HTMLElement) => {
      const index = lookupEl(el);
      if (index === -1) {
        if (debug)
          console.log(
            `Tried to remove an element that did not exist. Skipping...`
          );
        return;
      }

      const elHash = hash(el.outerHTML);
      elements[index]?.mirror?.remove();
      delete hashes[elHash];

      if (debug) console.log(`Removed style node ${el.outerHTML}`);
    };

    const observer = new MutationObserver((mutations) => {
      mutations.forEach((mutation) => {
        if (mutation.type === "childList") {
          mutation.addedNodes.forEach((node) => {
            if (
              node.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE ||
              node.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE
            ) {
              const el =
                node.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE
                  ? node.parentElement
                  : (node as HTMLElement);

              if (el && el.matches(styleSelector)) {
                defer(() => addEl(el));
              }
            }
          });

          mutation.removedNodes.forEach((node) => {
            if (
              node.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE ||
              node.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE
            ) {
              const el =
                node.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE
                  ? node.parentElement
                  : (node as HTMLElement);

              if (el && el.matches(styleSelector)) {
                defer(() => removeEl(el));
              }
            }
          });
        }
      });
    });

    const parentDocument = win!.parent.document;

    const collectedStyles = collectStyles(parentDocument);
    const hrefs: string[] = [];
    let stylesLoaded = 0;

    // Sync attributes for the HTML tag
    const parentHtml = parentDocument.getElementsByTagName("html")[0];
    syncAttributes(parentHtml, doc.documentElement);

    // Sync attributes for the Body tag
    const parentBody = parentDocument.getElementsByTagName("body")[0];
    syncAttributes(parentBody, doc.body);

    // Set iframe body background to transparent
    doc.body.style.backgroundColor = "transparent";

    // Copy CSS custom properties (variables) from parent html element
    const parentComputedStyle = win.parent.getComputedStyle(parentHtml);
    for (let i = 0; i < parentComputedStyle.length; i++) {
      const property = parentComputedStyle[i];
      if (property.startsWith("--")) {
        const value = parentComputedStyle.getPropertyValue(property);
        doc.documentElement.style.setProperty(property, value);
        doc.body.style.setProperty(property, value);
      }
    }

    // Copy font-related classes from parent body
    parentBody.classList.forEach((className) => {
      if (
        className.includes("font-") ||
        className === "antialiased" ||
        className.includes("__variable")
      ) {
        doc.body.classList.add(className);
      }
    });

    Promise.all(
      collectedStyles.map(async (styleNode) => {
        if (styleNode.nodeName === "LINK") {
          const linkHref = (styleNode as HTMLLinkElement).href;

          // Don't process link elements with identical hrefs more than once
          if (hrefs.indexOf(linkHref) > -1) {
            return;
          }
          hrefs.push(linkHref);
        }

        const mirror = await mirrorEl(styleNode);
        if (!mirror) return;

        elements.push({ original: styleNode, mirror });
        return mirror;
      })
    ).then((mirrorStyles) => {
      const filtered = mirrorStyles.filter(
        (el) => typeof el !== "undefined"
      ) as HTMLStyleElement[];

      filtered.forEach((mirror) => {
        mirror.onload = () => {
          stylesLoaded = stylesLoaded + 1;
          if (stylesLoaded >= elements.length) onStylesLoaded();
        };
        mirror.onerror = () => {
          console.warn(`AutoFrame couldn't load a stylesheet`);
          stylesLoaded = stylesLoaded + 1;
          if (stylesLoaded >= elements.length) onStylesLoaded();
        };
      });

      // Reset HTML (inside the promise) so in case running twice (React Strict Mode)
      doc.head.innerHTML = "";

      // Inject initial values in bulk
      doc.head.append(...filtered);

      observer.observe(parentDocument.head, { childList: true, subtree: true });

      filtered.forEach((el) => {
        const elHash = hash(el.outerHTML);
        hashes[elHash] = true;
      });
    });

    return () => {
      observer.disconnect();
    };
  }, [debug, doc, onStylesLoaded, win]);

  return <>{children}</>;
};

export type AutoFrameProps = {
  children: ReactNode;
  className: string;
  debug?: boolean;
  id?: string;
  onReady?: () => void;
  onNotReady?: () => void;
  style?: React.CSSProperties;
  pointerEventsEnabled?: boolean;
} & React.IframeHTMLAttributes<HTMLIFrameElement>;

type AutoFrameContext = {
  document?: Document;
  window?: Window;
};

export const autoFrameContext = createContext<AutoFrameContext>({});

export const useFrame = () => useContext(autoFrameContext);

const AutoFrame = forwardRef<HTMLIFrameElement, AutoFrameProps>(
  (
    {
      children,
      className,
      debug,
      id,
      onReady = () => {},
      onNotReady = () => {},
      style,
      pointerEventsEnabled = true,
      ...props
    },
    ref
  ) => {
    const [loaded, setLoaded] = useState(false);
    const [ctx, setCtx] = useState<AutoFrameContext>({});
    const [mountTarget, setMountTarget] = useState<HTMLElement | null>();
    const [stylesLoaded, setStylesLoaded] = useState(false);

    const handleLoad = useCallback(() => setLoaded(true), []);
    const handleStylesLoaded = useCallback(() => setStylesLoaded(true), []);

    const iframeStyle = useMemo(
      () => ({
        pointerEvents: pointerEventsEnabled ? ("all" as const) : ("none" as const),
        ...style,
      }),
      [pointerEventsEnabled, style]
    );

    useEffect(() => {
      if (ref && "current" in ref && ref.current) {
        const doc = ref.current.contentDocument;
        const win = ref.current.contentWindow;

        setCtx({
          document: doc || undefined,
          window: win || undefined,
        });

        setMountTarget(ref.current.contentDocument?.getElementById("frame-root"));

        if (doc && win && stylesLoaded) {
          onReady();
        } else {
          onNotReady();
        }
      }
      // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
    }, [ref, loaded, stylesLoaded]);

    return (
      <IframeAdapter
        {...props}
        className={className}
        id={id}
        data-testid="auto-frame"
        srcDoc='<!DOCTYPE html><html><head></head><body><div id="frame-root" data-autoform-entry style="display: contents;"></div></body></html>'
        frameRef={ref}
        style={iframeStyle}
        onLoad={handleLoad}
      >
        <autoFrameContext.Provider value={ctx}>
          {loaded && mountTarget && (
            <CopyHostStyles debug={debug} onStylesLoaded={handleStylesLoaded}>
              {createPortal(children, mountTarget)}
            </CopyHostStyles>
          )}
        </autoFrameContext.Provider>
      </IframeAdapter>
    );
  }
);

AutoFrame.displayName = "AutoFrame";
export default AutoFrame;
✅ 2) props-panel.tsx (full replacement)
You already import z (good), but you import getBaseType from an internal utils path that doesn’t export a function compatible with your usage. This version removes that import and defines a safe local getBaseType that unwraps Zod wrappers and returns a ZodFirstPartyTypeKind.

Path: web/components/ui/ui-builder/internal/props-panel.tsx

// web/components/ui/ui-builder/internal/props-panel.tsx
import React, { useCallback, useMemo } from "react";
import { z } from "zod";
import { useLayerStore } from "@/lib/ui-builder/store/layer-store";
import { useEditorStore } from "@/lib/ui-builder/store/editor-store";
import {
  ComponentRegistry,
  ComponentLayer,
} from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/types";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import AutoForm from "@/components/ui/auto-form";
import { generateFieldOverrides } from "@/lib/ui-builder/store/editor-utils";
import { addDefaultValues } from "@/lib/ui-builder/store/schema-utils";
// ❌ REMOVE this import (it isn't a callable function for your usage):
// import { getBaseType } from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/internal/form-fields/classname-control/utils";
import { isVariableReference } from "@/lib/ui-builder/utils/variable-resolver";
import { resolveVariableReferences } from "@/lib/ui-builder/utils/variable-resolver";

/** Safely unwrap Zod wrappers (effects/optional/default/etc.) to the underlying base type */
function getBaseType(schema: z.ZodAny): z.ZodFirstPartyTypeKind | undefined {
  let s: any = schema;
  // unwrap common wrappers: ZodDefault, ZodOptional, ZodEffects, etc.
  while (s?._def?.schema) {
    s = s._def.schema;
  }
  return s?._def?.typeName as z.ZodFirstPartyTypeKind | undefined;
}

interface PropsPanelProps {
  className?: string;
}

const PropsPanel: React.FC<PropsPanelProps> = React.memo(({ className }) => {
  const selectedLayerId = useLayerStore((state) => state.selectedLayerId);
  const findLayerById = useLayerStore((state) => state.findLayerById);
  const removeLayer = useLayerStore((state) => state.removeLayer);
  const duplicateLayer = useLayerStore((state) => state.duplicateLayer);
  const updateLayer = useLayerStore((state) => state.updateLayer);
  const addComponentLayer = useLayerStore((state) => state.addComponentLayer);
  const componentRegistry = useEditorStore((state) => state.registry);
  const selectedLayer = findLayerById(selectedLayerId);

  const handleAddComponentLayer = useCallback(
    (layerType: string, parentLayerId: string, addPosition?: number) => {
      addComponentLayer(layerType, parentLayerId, addPosition);
    },
    [addComponentLayer]
  );

  const handleDeleteLayer = useCallback(
    (layerId: string) => {
      removeLayer(layerId);
    },
    [removeLayer]
  );

  const handleDuplicateLayer = useCallback(() => {
    if (selectedLayer) {
      duplicateLayer(selectedLayer.id);
    }
  }, [selectedLayer, duplicateLayer]);

  const handleUpdateLayer = useCallback(
    (
      id: string,
      props: Record<string, any>,
      rest?: Partial<Omit<ComponentLayer, "props">>
    ) => {
      updateLayer(id, props, rest);
    },
    [updateLayer]
  );

  //first check if selectedLayer.type is a valid key in componentRegistry
  if (
    selectedLayer &&
    !componentRegistry[selectedLayer.type as keyof typeof componentRegistry]
  ) {
    return null;
  }

  return (
    <div className={className}>
      {selectedLayer && (
        <>
          <Title />
          <h3 className="text-base font-medium mb-4">
            Type: {selectedLayer.type.replaceAll("_", "")}
          </h3>
        </>
      )}

      {!selectedLayer && (
        <>
          <h2 className="text-xl font-semibold mb-2">Component Properties</h2>
          <p>No component selected</p>
        </>
      )}
      {selectedLayer && (
        <ComponentPropsAutoForm
          key={selectedLayer.id}
          componentRegistry={componentRegistry}
          selectedLayerId={selectedLayer.id}
          removeLayer={handleDeleteLayer}
          duplicateLayer={handleDuplicateLayer}
          updateLayer={handleUpdateLayer}
          addComponentLayer={handleAddComponentLayer}
        />
      )}
    </div>
  );
});
PropsPanel.displayName = "PropsPanel";
export default PropsPanel;

interface ComponentPropsAutoFormProps {
  selectedLayerId: string;
  componentRegistry: ComponentRegistry;
  removeLayer: (id: string) => void;
  duplicateLayer: (id: string) => void;
  updateLayer: (
    id: string,
    props: Record<string, any>,
    rest?: Partial<Omit<ComponentLayer, "props">>
  ) => void;
  addComponentLayer: (
    layerType: string,
    parentLayerId: string,
    addPosition?: number
  ) => void;
}

const EMPTY_ZOD_SCHEMA = z.object({});
const EMPTY_FORM_VALUES = {};

const ComponentPropsAutoForm: React.FC<ComponentPropsAutoFormProps> = ({
  selectedLayerId,
  componentRegistry,
  removeLayer,
  duplicateLayer,
  updateLayer,
  addComponentLayer,
}) => {
  const findLayerById = useLayerStore((state) => state.findLayerById);
  const revisionCounter = useEditorStore((state) => state.revisionCounter);
  const selectedLayer = findLayerById(selectedLayerId) as
    | ComponentLayer
    | undefined;
  const isPage = useLayerStore((state) => state.isLayerAPage(selectedLayerId));
  const allowPagesCreation = useEditorStore(
    (state) => state.allowPagesCreation
  );
  const allowPagesDeletion = useEditorStore(
    (state) => state.allowPagesDeletion
  );

  // Retrieve the appropriate schema from componentRegistry
  const { schema } = useMemo(() => {
    if (
      selectedLayer &&
      componentRegistry[selectedLayer.type as keyof typeof componentRegistry]
    ) {
      return componentRegistry[
        selectedLayer.type as keyof typeof componentRegistry
      ];
    }
    return { schema: EMPTY_ZOD_SCHEMA }; // Fallback schema
  }, [selectedLayer, componentRegistry]);

  const handleDeleteLayer = useCallback(() => {
    removeLayer(selectedLayerId);
  }, [removeLayer, selectedLayerId]);

  const handleDuplicateLayer = useCallback(() => {
    duplicateLayer(selectedLayerId);
  }, [duplicateLayer, selectedLayerId]);

  const onParsedValuesChange = useCallback(
    (
      parsedValues: z.infer<typeof schema> & {
        children?: string | { layerType: string; addPosition: number };
      }
    ) => {
      const { children, ...dataProps } = parsedValues;

      // Preserve variable references by merging with original props
      const preservedProps: Record<string, any> = {};
      if (selectedLayer) {
        // Start with all original props to preserve any that aren't in the form update
        Object.assign(preservedProps, selectedLayer.props);

        // Then update only the props that came from the form, preserving variable references
        Object.keys(dataProps as Record<string, any>).forEach((key) => {
          const originalValue = selectedLayer.props[key];
          const newValue = (dataProps as Record<string, any>)[key];
          const fieldDef =
            "shape" in schema && (schema as any).shape
              ? (schema as any).shape[key]
              : undefined;
          const baseType = fieldDef ? getBaseType(fieldDef as z.ZodAny) : undefined;

          if (isVariableReference(originalValue)) {
            // Keep the variable reference - the form should not override variable bindings
            preservedProps[key] = originalValue;
          } else {
            // Handle date serialization
            if (
              baseType === z.ZodFirstPartyTypeKind.ZodDate &&
              newValue instanceof Date
            ) {
              preservedProps[key] = newValue.toISOString();
            } else {
              preservedProps[key] = newValue;
            }
          }
        });
      }

      if (typeof children === "string") {
        updateLayer(selectedLayerId, preservedProps, { children: children });
      } else if (children && (children as any).layerType) {
        updateLayer(selectedLayerId, preservedProps, {
          children: selectedLayer?.children,
        });
        addComponentLayer(
          (children as any).layerType,
          selectedLayerId,
          (children as any).addPosition
        );
      } else {
        updateLayer(selectedLayerId, preservedProps);
      }
    },
    [updateLayer, selectedLayerId, selectedLayer, addComponentLayer, schema]
  );

  // Memoize variables to avoid accessing store state on every render
  const variables = useLayerStore((state) => state.variables);

  // Prepare values for AutoForm, converting enum values to strings as select elements only accept string values
  const formValues = useMemo(() => {
    if (!selectedLayer) return EMPTY_FORM_VALUES;

    // First resolve variable references to get display values
    const resolvedProps = resolveVariableReferences(
      selectedLayer.props,
      variables
    );

    const transformedProps: Record<string, any> = {};
    const schemaShape =
      "shape" in schema && (schema as any).shape
        ? ((schema as any).shape as z.ZodRawShape)
        : undefined;

    if (schemaShape) {
      for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(resolvedProps)) {
        const fieldDef = (schemaShape as any)[key];
        if (fieldDef) {
          const baseType = getBaseType(fieldDef as z.ZodAny);
          if (baseType === z.ZodFirstPartyTypeKind.ZodEnum) {
            transformedProps[key] =
              typeof value === "string" ? value : String(value);
          } else if (baseType === z.ZodFirstPartyTypeKind.ZodDate) {
            if (value instanceof Date) {
              transformedProps[key] = value;
            } else if (typeof value === "string" || typeof value === "number") {
              const date = new Date(value);
              transformedProps[key] = isNaN(date.getTime()) ? undefined : date;
            } else {
              transformedProps[key] = undefined;
            }
          } else {
            transformedProps[key] = value;
          }
        } else {
          transformedProps[key] = value;
        }
      }
    } else {
      // Fallback if schema shape isn't available: copy resolved props as is
      Object.assign(transformedProps, resolvedProps);
    }

    return { ...transformedProps, children: selectedLayer.children };
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, [selectedLayer, schema, revisionCounter]); // Include revisionCounter to detect undo/redo changes

  const autoFormSchema = useMemo(() => {
    // Only pass ZodObject schemas to addDefaultValues, otherwise return the original schema
    if ("shape" in schema && typeof (schema as any).shape === "object") {
      try {
        return addDefaultValues(schema as any, formValues);
      } catch (error) {
        console.warn("Failed to add default values to schema:", error);
        return schema;
      }
    }
    return schema;
  }, [schema, formValues]);

  const autoFormFieldConfig = useMemo(() => {
    if (!selectedLayer) return undefined;
    return generateFieldOverrides(componentRegistry, selectedLayer);
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, [componentRegistry, selectedLayer, selectedLayer?.props]);

  const formKey = useMemo(() => {
    return `${selectedLayerId}-${revisionCounter}`;
  }, [selectedLayerId, revisionCounter]);

  if (
    !selectedLayer ||
    !componentRegistry[selectedLayer.type as keyof typeof componentRegistry]
  ) {
    return null;
  }

  return (
    <AutoForm
      key={formKey}
      formSchema={autoFormSchema}
      values={formValues}
      onParsedValuesChange={onParsedValuesChange}
      fieldConfig={autoFormFieldConfig}
      className="space-y-4 mt-4"
    >
      {(!isPage || allowPagesCreation) && (
        <Button
          type="button"
          variant="secondary"
          className="mt-4 w-full"
          onClick={handleDuplicateLayer}
          data-testid={`button-Duplicate ,${isPage ? "Page" : "Component"}`}
        >
          Duplicate {isPage ? "Page" : "Component"}
        </Button>
      )}
      {(!isPage || allowPagesDeletion) && (
        <Button
          type="button"
          variant="destructive"
          className="mt-4 w-full"
          onClick={handleDeleteLayer}
          data-testid={`button-Delete ,${isPage ? "Page" : "Component"}`}
        >
          Delete {isPage ? "Page" : "Component"}
        </Button>
      )}
    </AutoForm>
  );
};

ComponentPropsAutoForm.displayName = "ComponentPropsAutoForm";

const nameForLayer = (layer: ComponentLayer) => {
  return layer.name || layer.type.replaceAll("_", "");
};

const Title = React.memo(() => {
  const selectedLayer = useLayerStore((state) => {
    const selectedLayerId = state.selectedLayerId;
    return selectedLayerId ? state.findLayerById(selectedLayerId) : null;
  });

  return (
    <h2 className="text-xl font-semibold mb-2">
      {selectedLayer ? nameForLayer(selectedLayer) : ""} Properties
    </h2>
  );
});

Title.displayName = "Title";

web/app/engine-status/page.tsx

// app/engine-status/page.tsx
import React from "react";

type Health = { status: string; service?: string; version?: string };

async function getHealth(): Promise<{ ok: boolean; data?: Health; error?: string }> {
  try {
    const res = await fetch("http://127.0.0.1:5173/health", { cache: "no-store" });
    if (!res.ok) return { ok: false, error: `HTTP ${res.status}` };
    const data = (await res.json()) as Health;
    return { ok: true, data };
  } catch (e: any) {
    return { ok: false, error: String(e?.message ?? e) };
  }
}

export default async function EngineStatusPage() {
  const health = await getHealth();

  return (
    <main className="min-h-[60vh] flex items-center justify-center p-8">
      <div className="max-w-xl w-full rounded-xl border p-6">
        <h1 className="text-2xl font-semibold mb-4">Engine Health</h1>
        {health.ok ? (
          <pre className="bg-black/5 rounded p-4 text-sm">
{JSON.stringify(health.data, null, 2)}
          </pre>
        ) : (
          <div className="text-red-500">
            Couldn’t reach daemon: <span className="font-mono">{health.error}</span>
          </div>
        )}
        <div className="mt-4 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
          If this shows <code>{"{ status: \"ok\" }"}</code> your Rust daemon is up.
        </div>
      </div>
    </main>
  );
}
Visit http://localhost:3000/engine-status. If you see { "status": "ok" }, you’re golden.

– Firefox: add localhost to “Don’t check on these sites” in Grammarly settings.

2) “CodeBlock is not exported from '@/components/ui/ui-builder/components/codeblock'”
The registry is importing a named export CodeBlock from that path, but the file either doesn’t exist or doesn’t export CodeBlock. Let’s just add a compatible drop-in.

Create this file (or replace its contents if it exists):

web/components/ui/ui-builder/components/codeblock.tsx

"use client";

import * as React from "react";
import { Prism as SyntaxHighlighter } from "react-syntax-highlighter";
import { coldarkDark } from "react-syntax-highlighter/dist/cjs/styles/prism";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

export type CodeBlockProps = {
  code: string;
  language?: string;   // e.g. "tsx", "typescript", "javascript", "json", etc.
  className?: string;
  showLineNumbers?: boolean;
  wrapLongLines?: boolean;
};

export function CodeBlock({
  code,
  language = "tsx",
  className,
  showLineNumbers = true,
  wrapLongLines = true,
}: CodeBlockProps) {
  // Guard against undefined to avoid runtime errors from SyntaxHighlighter
  const safe = typeof code === "string" ? code : String(code ?? "");

  return (
    <div className={cn("rounded-md border bg-background", className)}>
      <SyntaxHighlighter
        language={language}
        style={coldarkDark}
        showLineNumbers={showLineNumbers}
        wrapLongLines={wrapLongLines}
        customStyle={{
          margin: 0,
          background: "transparent",
          fontSize: 14,
          lineHeight: 1.6,
        }}
      >
        {safe}
      </SyntaxHighlighter>
    </div>
  );
}
That satisfies import { CodeBlock } from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/components/codeblock" in both code-panel.tsx and markdown.tsx.

If your imports were defaulted anywhere (rare), change them to a named import:

What you’re seeing in DevTools > Issues

Thought for 1m 15s
Great progress — the iframe warnings are gone. Your DOM shows clean <iframe …> elements with no rogue frameRef prop, so the adapter is doing its job ✅.

Here’s how to clear the remaining two categories of warnings you’re seeing.

1) “CodeBlock is not exported…” (import shape mismatch)
Your current codeblock.tsx only has a default export. Some files import it as a named export ({ CodeBlock }), which causes the warning.

Replace the whole file with this version (it keeps your styling and adds a named export as well):

"use client";

import React, { memo, useCallback, useMemo, useState } from "react";
import { Prism as SyntaxHighlighter } from "react-syntax-highlighter";
// NOTE: use ESM styles path (newer versions)
// Old CJS path was: dist/cjs/styles/prism
import { coldarkDark } from "react-syntax-highlighter/dist/esm/styles/prism";

import { CopyIcon, CheckIcon } from "lucide-react";
import { useCopyToClipboard } from "@/hooks/use-copy-to-clipboard";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";

type CodeBlockProps = {
  code: string;
  language?: string;
  showCopy?: boolean;
  className?: string;
};

const CodeBlockBase = ({
  code,
  language = "tsx",
  showCopy = true,
  className = "",
}: CodeBlockProps) => {
  const normalized = useMemo(() => (code ?? "").trim(), [code]);
  const { copy } = useCopyToClipboard();
  const [copied, setCopied] = useState(false);

  const handleCopy = useCallback(async () => {
    await copy(normalized);
    setCopied(true);
    setTimeout(() => setCopied(false), 1600);
  }, [copy, normalized]);

  return (
    <div className={`relative rounded-md overflow-hidden ${className}`}>
      {showCopy && (
        <div className="absolute right-2 top-2 z-10">
          <Button size="sm" variant="secondary" onClick={handleCopy}>
            {copied ? (
              <>
                <CheckIcon className="h-4 w-4 mr-1" />
                Copied
              </>
            ) : (
              <>
                <CopyIcon className="h-4 w-4 mr-1" />
                Copy
              </>
            )}
          </Button>
        </div>
      )}

      <SyntaxHighlighter
        language={language}
        style={coldarkDark}
        customStyle={{ margin: 0, padding: "16px", background: "transparent" }}
        codeTagProps={{ style: { fontSize: 13, lineHeight: 1.5 } }}
      >
        {normalized}
      </SyntaxHighlighter>
    </div>
  );
};

export const CodeBlock = memo(CodeBlockBase); // named export
export default CodeBlock;                     // default export (kept)
Now both of these will work anywhere:

import CodeBlock from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/components/codeblock";
// or
import { CodeBlock } from "@/components/ui/ui-builder/components/codeblock";
2) Tiptap “BubbleMenu is not exported from @tiptap/react” warnings
Your UI-builder registry expects Tiptap v2 APIs, but your project pulled v3 packages. You tried pinning v2.6.6 and got a peer conflict (your log shows @tiptap/core@2.26.2 requiring @tiptap/pm ^2.7.0).

Easiest fix (recommended): align all Tiptap packages to a v2 set
Run this from web/:

        @tiptap/extension-placeholder@2.26.2 \
        @tiptap/extension-color@2.26.2 \
        @tiptap/extension-text-style@2.26.2 \
        @tiptap/extension-underline@2.26.2 \
        @tiptap/extension-horizontal-rule@2.26.2 \
        @tiptap/extension-heading@2.26.2 \

These versions satisfy the peer constraints (notably @tiptap/pm >= 2.7.x).

3) “label for=… doesn’t match any element id” (accessibility warning)
You spotted two labels in the rendered DOM:

<label … for="_r_1a_-form-item">Class Name …</label>
<label … for="_r_3m_-form-item">Children …</label>
Those for values must match real input IDs. Because I don’t have your exact component code for those controls, here’s the drop-in pattern to apply inside your form field components (usually in the UI builder’s form-fields area):

import * as React from "react";

// inside your field component
const inputId = React.useId(); // unique per render tree

<label htmlFor={inputId} /* …classes */>Class Name<span className="text-destructive"> *</span></label>

<input
  id={inputId}
  /* the rest of your props: value, onChange, className, etc. */
/>
Anywhere you previously had <Label for="…">, switch to React’s htmlFor and wire it to the input’s id. If you’d like, paste the specific file(s) that render those two fields and I’ll return a fully edited version.

export const metadata: Metadata = {
  title: "Clipollo",
  description: "Community-hosted video platform",
};

export default function RootLayout({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
  return (
    <html
      lang="en"
      suppressHydrationWarning
      data-gramm="false"
      data-gramm_editor="false"
    >
      <body
        suppressHydrationWarning
        data-gramm="false"

3) Fix the “CodeBlock is not exported” warnings
Those warnings are coming from imports that expect a CodeBlock export from your local component. Let’s provide one that works with react-syntax-highlighter@15 and Prism styles.

Create/replace I:\…\web\components\ui\ui-builder\components\codeblock.tsx with:

// components/ui/ui-builder/components/codeblock.tsx
"use client";

import * as React from "react";
import { Prism as SyntaxHighlighter } from "react-syntax-highlighter";
// Use the ESM style path (works with v15+)
import { coldarkDark } from "react-syntax-highlighter/dist/esm/styles/prism";

export type CodeBlockProps = {
  language?: string;
  value?: string;
  className?: string;
  showLineNumbers?: boolean;
  wrapLongLines?: boolean;
};

/**
 * Local CodeBlock that ui-builder can import as either default or named export.
 * It uses Prism/coldarkDark to avoid the old CJS path that caused build errors.
 */
export function CodeBlock({
  language = "tsx",
  value = "",
  className,
  showLineNumbers = false,
  wrapLongLines = true,
}: CodeBlockProps) {
  return (
    <div className={className}>
      <SyntaxHighlighter
        language={language}
        style={coldarkDark}
        showLineNumbers={showLineNumbers}
        wrapLongLines={wrapLongLines}
        customStyle={{ margin: 0, background: "transparent" }}
      >
        {value}
      </SyntaxHighlighter>
    </div>
  );
}

// let both `import CodeBlock from ...` and `import { CodeBlock } from ...` work
export default CodeBlock;
No other edits are needed—code-panel.tsx and markdown.tsx will be satisfied as long as they import from the same path they did before.

You’re super close — Git just tripped over a nested repo.

error: 'engine/daemon/' does not have a commit checked out
means there’s a .git folder inside engine/daemon (and possibly in web/), so Git thinks those are separate repositories (submodules) and refuses to add them.

Fix: remove the nested .git folders (we want one monorepo)
Run these from:
I:\Documents\Projects\MyVideoPlatformProject\video-platform

:: 1) See any nested .git directories
dir /ad /s /b .git

:: 2) Remove the ones inside your project folders (NOT the top-level .git)
rmdir /s /q "engine\daemon\.git"
rmdir /s /q "engine\.git"       2>nul
rmdir /s /q "web\.git"          2>nul

:: (Optional) normalize line-endings warning
git config core.autocrlf true
If dir shows only the top-level .git (in video-platform\.git), then you’re good; otherwise remove any extra ones under engine\... or web\....

Stage, commit, push
git add .
git commit -m "chore: bootstrap repo (web + engine + docs)"

git branch -M main
git remote add origin https://github.com/<your-user>/<your-repo>.git
git push -u origin main
Why this happened
cargo new and some scaffolding tools sometimes run git init in the created folder. That leaves a .git inside engine\daemon (and occasionally web). For a single-repo setup, delete those nested .git directories.

If you actually wanted submodules
Totally possible, but then you’d:

git submodule add <repo-url> engine/daemon
Since we’re keeping everything together for now, removing the inner .git is simpler.

You have two good choices:

Option A — Make the workflow smart (recommended now)
Only build a thing when its folder changed. Also, don’t fail the whole pipeline if the optional web job fails while we’re still wiring it up.

Replace your .github/workflows/ci.yml with this version:

name: CI
on:
  push:
    branches: [ main ]
    paths:
      - 'engine/**'
      - 'web/**'
      - '.github/workflows/ci.yml'
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main ]
    paths:
      - 'engine/**'
      - 'web/**'
      - '.github/workflows/ci.yml'

jobs:
  engine:
    if: "contains(github.event.head_commit.message, '[skip-engine]') == false"
    name: Engine (Rust) build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    defaults:
      run:
        working-directory: engine/daemon
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Install Rust
        uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
        with:
          toolchain: stable

      - name: Cache cargo
        uses: actions/cache@v4
        with:
          path: |
            ~/.cargo/registry
            ~/.cargo/git
            engine/daemon/target
          key: ${{ runner.os }}-cargo-${{ hashFiles('engine/daemon/**/Cargo.lock') }}

      - name: Build
        run: cargo build --release

      - name: Test
        run: cargo test --all --verbose

  web:
    if: "github.event_name == 'pull_request' || contains(github.event.head_commit.message, '[skip-web]') == false"
    name: Web (Next.js) build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    continue-on-error: true   # Temporary while we stabilize the web build
    defaults:
      run:
        working-directory: web
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Setup Node
        uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: '20'
          cache: 'npm'
          cache-dependency-path: web/package-lock.json

      - name: Install deps
        run: npm ci

      - name: Lint
        run: npm run lint --if-present

      - name: Build
        run: npm run build --if-present
What this gives you right now:

CI won’t run the engine job unless engine/** changed (same idea for web and web/**).

The web job is non-blocking (continue-on-error: true) while we’re still integrating UI Builder, so your checks stay green when only Rust passes.

You can force-skip either side by putting [skip-web] or [skip-engine] in a commit message.

Push it:

cd /d "I:\Documents\Projects\MyVideoPlatformProject\video-platform"

Prevent the CI “red X” until the app builds
Your Actions failure earlier was because our workflow tries to build the web app and engine even though we haven’t committed the full code yet. Two easy ways to stop noisy failures:

Option A (recommended): Run each job only when its folder changed
Replace .github/workflows/ci.yml with this trimmed version:

name: CI

on:
  push:
  pull_request:

jobs:
  web:
    if: contains(github.event.head_commit.message, '[skip web]') == false
    name: Web (Next.js) build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    # Only if the web folder changed
    if: |
      contains(github.event_name, 'pull_request') && 
      (github.event.pull_request.changed_files > 0) ||
      (github.event_name == 'push')
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Skip if web/ didn't change
        id: path_filter
        uses: dorny/paths-filter@v3
        with:
          filters: |
            web:
              - 'web/**'
      - name: Stop (web unchanged)
        if: steps.path_filter.outputs.web != 'true'
        run: echo "web/ unchanged — skipping."
      - name: Setup Node
        if: steps.path_filter.outputs.web == 'true'
        uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: '20'
          cache: 'npm'
          cache-dependency-path: web/package-lock.json
      - name: Build web
        if: steps.path_filter.outputs.web == 'true'
        working-directory: web
        run: |
          npm ci
          npm run build

  engine:
    name: Engine (Rust) build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Skip if engine/ didn't change
        id: path_filter
        uses: dorny/paths-filter@v3
        with:
          filters: |
            engine:
              - 'engine/**'
      - name: Stop (engine unchanged)
        if: steps.path_filter.outputs.engine != 'true'
        run: echo "engine/ unchanged — skipping."
      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
        if: steps.path_filter.outputs.engine == 'true'
      - name: Build engine
        if: steps.path_filter.outputs.engine == 'true'
        working-directory: engine/daemon
        run: cargo build --locked --verbose
This makes each job noop if its folder didn’t change, so the workflow goes green when we’re just pushing docs or meta changes.

Option B (quickest): Allow the web job to “continue on error”
If you prefer, add this line under the web job:

continue-on-error: true

You said:
where is the continue-on-error: option that i change to true  ?
ChatGPT said:
Add it in your GitHub Actions workflow, right under the job you want to tolerate failures for (the “web” job). File path:

video-platform/.github/workflows/ci.yml
Find the web job and add continue-on-error: true at the same indentation level as runs-on. For example:

name: CI
on:
  push:
  pull_request:

jobs:
  web:
    name: Web (Next.js) build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    continue-on-error: true   # <-- add this line here
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: '20'
          cache: 'npm'
          cache-dependency-path: web/package-lock.json
      - name: Build web
        working-directory: web
        run: |
          npm ci
          npm run build

  engine:
    name: Engine (Rust) build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
      - name: Build engine
        working-directory: engine/daemon
        run: cargo build --locked --verbose
Notes:

You can also put continue-on-error: true on an individual step if you only want to ignore one command’s failure:

- name: Build web
  continue-on-error: true
  run: npm run build
Job-level continue-on-error will mark the job as “skipped/neutral” if it fails, allowing the workflow to succeed overall. Use it as a temporary measure; it hides real build breakages.

After editing, commit and push:

git add .github/workflows/ci.yml
git commit -m "ci: allow web job failure temporarily"
git push
Then go to GitHub → Actions and re-run the workflow (or push again) to see it pass.

How to add these to your project
Unzip into your repo root so the folders line up like:

I:\Documents\Projects\MyVideoPlatformProject\video-platform\
  docs\CONTEXT_SEED.md
  docs\TODAY_SUMMARY_2025-10-02.md
  docs\NEXT_STEPS.md
  web\app\engine-status\page.tsx
Commit and push (ideally from a feature branch):

cd /d "I:\Documents\Projects\MyVideoPlatformProject\video-platform"

git switch -c docs/daily-2025-10-02

git add docs web/app/engine-status/page.tsx
git commit -m "docs: add daily summary, context seed, next steps + engine-status page"
git push -u origin docs/daily-2025-10-02
Open a PR on GitHub from docs/daily-2025-10-02 → main and merge when ready.
